# Matching workflow

Functions are matched by hand, by a person or an AI agent, **one function per
commit**, and every commit goes through one validation gate, `decomp.py done`.
The retired bulk matcher (`decomp.py auto`) left drafts that `next` still
shows as a starting point.

```mermaid
flowchart TD
    disc["Retail disc: SCUS_945.70 + CRASHBSH.DAT overlays"] --> split["splat: one asm file per function; C files are INCLUDE_ASM lines"]
    split --> next["decomp.py next: pick one function, show its asm + m2c draft"]
    next --> unit["Check the asm's style fits the file's compiler"]
    unit --> write["Write C in place of its INCLUDE_ASM line"]
    write --> diff{"make diff: same instructions?"}
    diff -. "no: fix the STAGE it reports (structure, then instructions, then registers)" .-> write
    diff -. "stuck at the registers stage" .-> permute["make permute"]
    permute -. "found a fix" .-> write
    permute -. "still differs" .-> block["decomp.py block: park it, best C kept under NON_MATCHING"]
    diff -- "yes" --> g1

    subgraph gate ["decomp.py done gate"]
        direction TB
        g1["Scope: only this function's INCLUDE_ASM replaced; other C edited only at its calls"] --> g2["Lint: no m2c leftovers, only C (no inline asm)"]
        g2 --> g3["Function: compiled words equal the target"]
        g3 --> g4["Full build: clean rebuild of the exe and all 15 overlays is byte-identical"]
    end

    gate -. "any check fails" .-> write
    g4 -- "all pass" --> commit["One commit per function: Match NAME; identical copies matched too; dashboard refreshed"]
    hook["pre-commit hook"] -. "refuses matches made any other way" .-> commit
    commit --> quality["Later passes: named, typed, placed in its original file"]
    quality --> goal(["Source-only Crash Bash: all C, no INCLUDE_ASM, byte-identical"])
```

## The loop

All commands run through `tools/dk` (Docker).

1. **Pick:** `tools/dk python3 tools/scripts/decomp.py next`
   prints the target, its callers, the asm and a draft (m2c, or an earlier
   compiling draft). `next --func NAME` picks a specific one, `--seg REGEX`
   restricts to a segment (`overlays/ovl62`, ...). From about 600 bytes up,
   also read [Big functions](#big-functions).
2. **Check the unit.** Compare the asm's style with its file's compiler
   settings: every global store as `lui $at` + `sw`, or jump delay slots
   filled from a store, is gcc 2.7.2 through ASPSX, not `main.c`'s gcc 2.8.1
   (see the Diff patterns row for `func_8002D488`). Code in the wrong unit
   never matches; it needs a split, with per-unit settings in the Makefile
   ([ARCHITECTURE.md](ARCHITECTURE.md), Build pipeline).
3. **Evidence first, then write C.** Before writing anything, gather
   evidence (see [Evidence first](#evidence-first)). Then replace the
   function's `INCLUDE_ASM` line in its source file with C. Use real types
   from `include/` (add structs there, see [CONVENTIONS.md](CONVENTIONS.md)).
   Don't leave m2c leftovers.
4. **Diff:** `tools/dk make diff FUNC=NAME` builds that file and shows
   the target and compiled code aligned (an extra instruction is one `+`
   row, not a shift of everything after it), with runs of identical rows
   cut. Marks: `!` operands differ, `~` moved (statement order), `-`
   target only, `+` compiled only. A `KINDS` line counts them (register,
   operand, moved, missing, extra) for [Classify the
   diff](#classify-the-diff-then-work-through-the-list). It stops after 40
   rows; for more, run
   `tools/dk sh -c 'DIFF_ROWS=300 python3 tools/scripts/decomp.py try NAME'`.
   On a match it also links the binary: `LINK ok`, or `LINK differs` with
   where (data or rodata the C adds or moves, or a jump-table entry, which
   the instruction diff doesn't compare; `done` would refuse it).
   Its last line, `STAGE NAME stage n/total`, says how far the function has
   got. Fix the stages in order, since later fixes don't hold while an
   earlier stage is wrong:
   - `structure`: the length or the branch layout differs (control flow,
     loops, jump tables, early returns).
   - `instructions`: same skeleton, some opcodes differ (statement order,
     temps, types, casts).
   - `registers`: operands only.

   Look the symptom up in [Diff patterns](#diff-patterns), then
   [classify the diff and work through its list](#classify-the-diff-then-work-through-the-list).
   For register differences, also read the [cc1 dump](#why-does-a-register-differ).
   Note in your reasoning which items you tried.
5. **Stuck?** If the diff is a tie in instruction order, register choice or
   a merged tail, [read the compiler's rules](#reading-the-compilers-rules)
   for the pass that decides it first. [Use the compiler
   directly](#using-the-compiler-directly): dump the passes for the
   function, find the first pass that goes wrong, and try C shapes in a
   small test file compiled with cc1. Run the permuter only at the
   `registers` stage, after the evidence step, the checklist for that kind
   of diff, the cc1 dump and that reading:
   `tools/dk make permute FUNC=NAME MINUTES=10`. When it finds something,
   port it by hand and add the general rule to [Diff patterns](#diff-patterns).
   Still stuck: restore the `INCLUDE_ASM` line, keep the best attempt under
   `#ifndef NON_MATCHING`, and run `decomp.py block NAME --note "STAGE line;
   what differs"`. Then move on.
6. **Validate and commit:**
   `tools/dk python3 tools/scripts/decomp.py done NAME --commit --note "what made it match"`

To run this loop for several functions at once, see [tools/swarm](../tools/swarm/README.md):
Claude workers do steps 1-5 in their own clones, and one integrator does step 6 for each.
Big functions go to big-mode workers there, in resumable sessions.

## Big functions

A function of 600 bytes or more rarely matches in one sitting, and the
permuter's random edits get nowhere on hundreds of instructions. Work on it
block by block over several sessions, keeping the best C between them:

- **Work and measure with `decomp.py regions NAME`.** `try`'s `STAGE`
  count compares instruction i with instruction i, so one extra instruction
  makes everything after it a diff, and the count can rise while the C gets
  closer. `regions` aligns the two streams and scores the target's
  basic blocks one by one (`ok`, `operands`, `differ`), with each differing
  block's C lines and its side-by-side diff (`--block N` for another one,
  `--no-lines` to skip the C-line build). It lists the first 12 differing
  blocks (`--all` for every block); after an edit, `--changed` lists only
  the blocks whose status changed since the last `regions` call. Its last line, `REGIONS NAME
  blocks m/n insns e/N paired p/N ...`, is the progress measure: more
  `paired` (target instructions aligned by opcode: the structure), then
  more identical blocks, then more exact instructions. Fix the first
  differing block (its missing or extra instructions before its operands),
  re-run, repeat; undo a change that loses blocks. A register change can
  cascade through every later block, so read `paired` and the block list
  together. `STAGE` still says when only registers are left (the
  permuter's stage).
- **Check the unit first** (step 2). `func_8002D4F4` (2360 bytes, the
  largest in `main.exe`) is in a gcc 2.7.2 unit; its last diffs were the unit's
  compiler flags and a reorg difference, not its C.
- **Type the data before matching.** Put structs for everything it touches
  in the owned header, from `include/bash/`, the prototype debug types and
  the leaked headers. m2c's offset casts and `u8 *` arithmetic cause most of
  a big function's diff.
- **Fix the function's own declarations.** A wrong parameter type in its
  prototype or an `extern` elsewhere shifts code all through it; change
  them in place (`done`'s full build catches a caller that stops
  matching), not through a macro or wrapper named after the function.
- **Look for helpers and siblings.** A run of instructions repeated inside
  the function, or shared with a matched function, is usually a
  `static inline` helper or a macro (see the pop/push rows in Diff
  patterns; `func_80019A60` gained 8 blocks from one shared draw helper).
  `decomp.py similar NAME` ranks matched functions by shared code (`sim`:
  a sibling to start from; `in`: a helper whose code is inside this one),
  and `decomp.py dups` and the callers and callees in
  `.decomp/functions.json` find more matched code to copy from.
- **Split a variable that changes register.** If `regions` shows one
  variable in different registers in different blocks, the original
  probably had several: `decomp.py splitvars NAME VAR` compiles every
  grouping of VAR's assignments and ranks them by region score (`--apply`
  writes the best; give the new `VAR_N` real names after). It found
  `func_80019A60`'s last 3 blocks in 10 s.
- **Permute late and narrow.** Only at the `registers` stage, and only on
  the C that differs: `decomp.py permute NAME --differing` (`make permute
  FUNC=NAME ARGS=--differing`) wraps the C lines of the blocks `regions`
  says differ in `PERM_RANDOMIZE`, widened to whole statements, so the
  permuter edits nothing else (`--block N`, `--lines A-B` to pick).
  On `func_80019A60`'s block 0 it found the match in 2 of 4 runs of 2-3
  minutes; the whole-function run needed 15 minutes to get part of the way.
  It is random, so rerun a miss. The swarm uses it for big functions.
- **The permuter's speed.** `decomp.py permute` gives decomp-permuter less to do per candidate
  (2026-10-20). Measured 2x to 7x as many candidates a second, more on small functions:
  `func_8002E584` went from 10.5 to 73 a second, `func_8007F314` (5772 B) from 3.5 to 6.1.
  - Its `base.c` holds the file's own text before the function: externs, macros, other
    functions as prototypes and inline helpers whole. A base of the `#include`s alone didn't
    compile a function using the file's own externs.
  - It's then pruned to the declarations the function uses (e.g. 137 KB to 5 KB). The
    pruning is kept only if it compiles to the same `.text`.
  - Each candidate compiles through `tools/scripts/permuter_fast.py`. It runs the
    `compile.sh` pipeline with dmpsx, maspsx and aspsx_fill inside the worker, reads the
    include dirs from `/tmp`, and skips cpp when it would change nothing. Its first compile
    must equal bash's byte for byte, or it uses bash.
  - Its score compares relocations by address (2026-10-20). The target asm names a field by
    its own address (`D_8005A677`); our C writes `D_8005A614+0x63`. Both link the same, but the
    stock scorer charged each pair, so `func_800B5710` scored 20 with C that `try` called a
    match, and `--stop-on-zero` never fired. Addresses come from the name or from the
    run's `symbols.txt` (main, engine and the function's overlay).
  - `PERMUTER_PLAIN=1` runs the stock permuter on the full context instead (old scoring too).
  - Every run is recorded: a `PERMUTE-STATS` line, `.decomp/permuter.jsonl` here and
    `<swarm dir>/permuter.jsonl` in the swarm. `decomp.py permstats [FILE...]` and
    `swarm.mjs stats` sum them by size and mode: runs, CPU minutes, improved, matched and
    speed per job. Check them before changing how the permuter is used.
- **Keep the best C between sessions.** By hand: `decomp.py block NAME
  --note "<REGIONS line>; next: ..."` with the C under `#ifndef
  NON_MATCHING`, so the next session starts from it. In the swarm, big mode
  keeps a checkpoint per function, ranked by the region score, and starts
  each session from it ([tools/swarm](../tools/swarm/README.md), Big mode).


## Evidence first

Most matches come from what's already known, not from guessing. Before the
first diff:

- **Near-identical functions:** `tools/dk python3 tools/scripts/decomp.py dups`
  lists groups of identical functions and of near-identical ones ("constants
  differ"). If a member is matched, start from its C.
- **Similar functions:** `tools/dk python3 tools/scripts/decomp.py similar
  NAME` ranks matched functions by shared opcode 4-grams, with the file and
  line of their C: `sim` is the share the two have in common (a sibling
  written the same way), `in` the share of the other found inside NAME (a
  helper or macro it inlines). `--all` includes unmatched ones, siblings to
  do together. `func_8001EAA4`'s sibling `func_8001EDFC` comes first (0.62).
  Rerun it on a parked function: a twin matched later shows as 1.00, and
  the gate's copy of its C fails when the prototypes differ (`copy_failed`
  in `.decomp/state.json`), so port it by hand with the local types
  (`func_800BFEE0`, `func_800B7190`).
- **Matched C:** grep `src/` for the same globals, callees or shape (the same
  loop, the same struct offsets). A sibling's C often shows the idiom.
  Example: `Random` and `func_800155FC` share the seed step.
- **The same instructions elsewhere:** `decomp.py idiom 'move A, B; sll B, A, N'`
  finds an instruction pattern in the matched functions' asm, smallest first,
  and prints the C lines behind each hit from a `-g` build. Placeholders bind
  one operand each (two register placeholders are two registers), `*` is any
  operand, `...` a gap, `N(A)` a memory operand; `move` and `li` stand for
  splat's `addu`/`addiu` forms (the syntax is in `tools/scripts/idiom.py`).
  For `func_800B4048`'s copy before a shift, it finds `j = i & 3;` in
  `func_800B3740` and its siblings: the copy's destination lives on after the
  shift. `--all` searches unmatched functions too.
- **Names and types:** check `include/bash/*.h` and the leaked minigame
  headers (`bash/tank.h`, `pogo.h`, `arkenoid.h`, `polar.h`; see
  [ARCHITECTURE.md](ARCHITECTURE.md), Leaked source) for the function's
  prototype, its structs and the globals' types.
- **Earlier builds:** the prototype and demo discs ([PROTOTYPES.md](PROTOTYPES.md))
  hold earlier compiles of most functions. `decomp.py protos NAME` finds
  the function in each disc's exe, engine or minigame overlays by opcode
  similarity, and `decomp.py protos NAME --diff Sep_14` aligns that disc's
  copy with retail by opcode and register (relocated `%hi`/`%lo`, branch
  offsets and shift counts set aside, or every one shows up) and prints only
  the hunks that differ. `--words` adds the copy in full, immediates kept,
  and `--file F.c` aligns the build of a test copy (built as `try --file`
  builds it) with the disc's copy instead of retail: a shape can be tested
  against an earlier build whose source differed (`func_800B4048`'s Oct 9
  copy, with other percentages). Diffing
  two successive builds shows which source change made a pattern: in
  `func_8007F314` the Oct 9 build added case 0's 0x7000 test and, with it,
  retail's tail merge, which narrowed the search to that test's shape.

## Diff patterns

Look up what the diff shows. **Every session that learns something from a
match or a park adds a row here** (not only an AGENT_LOG paragraph).

Natural C first (CONVENTIONS.md, Matching rules). Many rows steer a pass
with C that has no other reason to be there, most often a `do { } while (0)`
wrapper (its loop notes end a cse block and weight refs by loop depth for
the allocators). Use one only after the natural changes: read the row for
the gcc rule it names and look for a natural change with the same effect
first. Rows that need inline asm are not allowed (only C).

| Symptom in the diff | Likely source change | Example |
|---|---|---|
| A u16 parameter gets an `andi 0xffff` the target lacks, before a `- 1` and a small mask | Do the arithmetic in u16: `arg0--; t = arg0 & 0xFFF;` (combine drops the zero-extension; `(arg0 - 1) & 0xFFF` keeps it) | `map_entity_address` |
| An index the target sign-extends from 16 bits (`sll 16; sra 14`) right after storing it to an s16 field, where an int parameter indexes unextended | Index by the field just stored (`Alist[anim->ALindex]`): cse reuses the truncated register instead of reloading | `GotoLinkAnim` |
| Count-down loop ending `bne v1, a1` with `a1 = -1`, and a `beqz` guard before it | `for (i = n - 1; i != -1; i--)`; `while (n--)` and `while (n-- != 0)` don't match. Fixed count: `for (i = 62; i != -1; i--)` | `func_800154C0`, `func_800154EC`; `func_80012E54`, `func_8001325C` |
| Loop exit test at the top, `j` back at the bottom | Leave the loop by `return` or `goto` past it ([below](#loops-gcc-rotates-but-the-target-doesnt)) | `func_80015780` |
| `lhu` + `sll 16` + `sra 16` for a field | Nothing to fix: the field is `s16` (combine reuses the shifted value, e.g. for the `srl 31` of a `/ 2`) | `func_8001C7FC` |
| gcc leaves a branch delay slot unfilled that the target fills (its `.set noreorder` block is missing) | A return register is live on the taken path, e.g. a `return f(...)` that makes v0 live at the shared exit | `func_8001C7FC` (parked) |
| `mfhi` result lands in a different register | Register-allocation preference ("pref HI_REG"); read the [cc1 dump](#why-does-a-register-differ) | `func_8001C7FC`, `func_8001C974` |
| Argument still in `a0`, never written, at a call | Pass the caller's argument straight through to the callee; m2c drops it | `func_8001CFE8`, `func_800C6828` |
| A constant stored to two globals, `li` scheduled before the first `lui` | Hold the constant in a local: `on = TRUE; A = on; B = on;` | `func_8002D0C8` |
| A 16-bit store of a constant >= 0x8000 loaded with `ori` (not `addiu` of a negative) | The variable or field is `u16`; as `s16` gcc loads the sign-extended value with `addiu` | `func_8008F068` (`D_8005A644 = 0x8008`) |
| m2c's draft steps a typed pointer by a byte count (`p += 0x24` on an `s32 *`) | m2c writes the asm's byte offset; step by elements (`p += 9`) or use a struct of that size. A plain `while` gives gcc's guarded `do-while` shape | `func_80095D88` |
| A pointer init placed after a hoisted loop constant (`li a0,-1` then `move s0,s1`) | It's a strength-reduced giv: write `&base[i]` with an `i` counter | `func_80015780` |
| Two pointer `addiu`s in the loop in the wrong order | Move `i++` before or after the other pointer's `++` | `func_80015780` |
| Loop constant not hoisted (stays in the loop) | The loop is too big for loop.c's threshold: compute a repeated address once into a local (`p = &pts[i]`) | `func_80015780` |
| Two pointers stepping in lockstep (one loaded through, one returned) | Two variables in the source, not one | `func_80015780` |
| `addiu a0,a0,0x6C` then fields at small offsets | A pointer to a sub-struct (`&ps->set`) | `func_80015C1C` |
| Vector 0 loaded with `lw` + `mtc2` | `gte_ldv0_w(xy, z)` (`bash/main.h`); load the arguments into locals first if the `lw`s come first | `func_8001D048` |
| `lw t4/t5/t6` + `ctc2` | Sony's `gte_SetRotMatrix` and siblings; pass one `MATRIX *` local to both `gte_SetRotMatrix` and `gte_SetTransMatrix` | `func_80015C1C`, `func_8002AEE0` |
| Other instructions right before a GTE command (`intpl`, `rtps`, `avsz3`, `nclip`, ...) | The `_b` macro (no nops): `gte_intpl_b()`, `gte_rtps_b()`, `gte_avsz3_b()`, `gte_nclip_b()` | `func_8001C0F0`, `func_800193A8` |
| `lw`/`or`/`sw` of a whole POLY word | Work on the word whole: `*(u32 *)&poly->r0 \| 0x02000000`, not `setSemiTrans` | `func_800177F4` (parked) |
| A commutative op's result lands in the other operand's register (`addu v1,v0,v1` vs `addu v0,v0,v1`) | local-alloc ties the result to the first operand that dies there; swap the operand order. If that moves the other registers, it's priority order: still open | `func_800155FC` (parked) |
| Callee-saved registers from s2 up (s0/s1 unused, t-registers free) in a leaf | The original pinned those locals (`register s32 xy asm("$20");`): normal allocation never skips s0. Inline asm isn't allowed now (only C), so such a function parks; this one predates the rule | `func_800193A8` |
| SXY stored at +0x10/+0x18/+0x20 straight off the primitive pointer | `gte_stsxy3_10(p)` (`bash/main.h`); no Sony macro has these offsets | `func_800193A8` |
| A tag word written whole, never loaded: `0x09000000 \| (ot[otz] & 0xFFFFFF)` | `poly->tag = 0x09000000 \| (ot[otz] & 0xFFFFFF); setaddr(ot + otz, poly);`, not `setlen` + `addPrim` (those give an `sb` and a read-modify-write) | `func_800193A8` |
| Several branches go to the later of two identical `j top; addiu` blocks; ours all go to the first | Each skip path `goto`s one label before the shared `p++` at the loop's end; repeated `p++; continue;` blocks get cross-jumped | `func_800193A8` |
| `base + 1` kept in its own register (`addiu t3,a2,1`), initialised after the hoisted constants | Read `base[1]` twice in the loop (test and use) so loop.c makes it a giv; a separate `count` pointer is initialised too early | `func_800193A8` |
| A late register copy of a value loaded earlier (`addu t0,v1,zero` before `rtps`) | Read the same value again at that point in the source (`n = runs[1]` after the test); CSE turns the reread into the copy | `func_800193A8` |
| Two identical `return x` blocks merged, but the target keeps the block before the second test (`bnez a; jr ra; ...; beq b,c → back`) where ours keeps it after (`beqz a → forward`) | Cross-jumping keeps the later copy, so write only the first one: put a label inside that block (`if (sub == 0) { found: return ext; }`) and `goto found` from the second test | `FindExtension` |
| An early exit returns a callee-saved register (`j exit; move v0,s0`) that holds an initialised `NULL` | One `return voice;` at the end, reached from that path; `return NULL` there gives `move v0,zero` | `PlaySfx` |
| Another function's body repeated inside this one | gcc 2.8 `-O2` doesn't inline, and the helper is often defined after the caller: write the code out in place (or a macro) | `PlaySfx` (`func_80022924`) |
| A pointer copied into a saved register in a branch delay slot, while nearby stores still use the original register | Assign the copy after those stores (`if (a) a->prev = v; voice = v;`); reorg moves it into the slot | `PlaySfx` |
| A loop's `return p` stub (`j exit; move v0,v1`) sits up after the first early return, and that first test is inverted (`beqz` → rest) | Join the later NULL checks into one condition: `if (a != NULL && (p = f(a)) != NULL) { loop }`, then one `return NULL`. Separate early returns, nested `if`s and `while`/`for`/`do` all give the other layout | `func_80025A48` |
| A global's `lui` hoisted to the entry (often into a delay slot) and shared by several later loads and stores of it | Use the global through a pointer local: `NAS **head = (NAS **)&D_8005AA34;`, then `*head` | `func_80026980` |
| `beqz b → far; j near` right after a first test (gcc threaded a jump through a repeated test) | Test both first and keep the rest inside: `if (a || b) { if (a) ... else ...; } else { ... }` | `func_80026980` |
| A loop tests a register that is always 0 (`beqz a2` after `move a2,zero`) and an id kept in a register | A helper written out in place with its parameters as locals (`id = 0x400; want = 0;` then `FindExtension`'s loop, `goto found`). Give each job its own local: reusing one for the switch key moved 8 operands | `func_800250B0` |
| A list loop's "append at the end" block sits before the loop (a backward `beqz` to it) | `while (p != NULL) { if (found) { ...; return; } if (p->next == NULL) { append; return; } p = p->next; }`, both exits inside; `for (;;)` with `break` puts it after the loop | `func_800110A8` |
| `x + 6 + y` computed as `x + (y + 6)`, or a nearby zero store scheduled before the loads | `x->size += 6 + y->size;`, and put the other store (`x->unk0 = 0`) after it in the source | `func_800110A8` |
| Stack frame bigger than the calls need (`addiu sp,-0x30` with `ra` at 0x28 and no locals used) | An unused local array of the missing size: `char unused[24];` | `func_800101E0` |
| `a * K + C + b * L` computed with the constant on the last term (`addiu` after both products), target adds it to the first | Two statements: `size = a * K + C; size += b * L;` (one expression is always reassociated) | `func_8002A650` |
| A fill loop sets up its pointer before the counter and the `-1` constant | Walk an explicit pointer (`p = Table[0]; ... p += 2;`), not `Table[i]` | `func_8002BD68`, `func_8002BBB4`, `func_8002B9A4` |
| A literal `0` argument passed from a register that holds a `0` result (`move a2,v0`), target uses `addu a2,zero,zero` | Assign the zero result in an `else` after the calls, not before them | `func_800280C0` |
| Signed `/ 0x1000` rounding temp in another register than the target's | Do the division as its own statement on a reused variable: `b = (b - a) * t; b /= 0x1000;` | `func_80015284` |
| Unlink writes `prev->next` from a saved `next` copy, target reloads `node->next` | Write `prev->next = node->next` (permuter find); a global's address held in a pointer local can need setting inside the outer loop (`pool = &D_80056844;`) | `func_8001828C` |
| `base + (off + K)` then `+ i * size` in two `addu`s into a saved register | Two statements: `rec = t + (t->off + K); rec += i * size;` | `func_8001682C` |
| A loop over records keeps one base pointer (fields at their offsets), ours splits it into two induction pointers with odd offsets (`-36(v1)`, `0(a0)`) | Build the loop with a label and `goto` (loop.c only optimises `for`/`while`/`do`), and hold a stored constant in a local | `func_80027F00` |
| A mask kept as `andi 0xF0` before a byte or halfword store (ours uses a hoisted `-16`), and the value loaded twice | Read the other half into a narrow local first and pass the result through an `s32` before the store: `lo = *p >> 4; v = ((*p << 4) & 0xF0) | lo; *p = v;` (permuter find) | `func_80015518`, `func_80015554` |

| After you move invariant statements out of a loop, a constant used in it (`li v0,-1`) is hoisted too and spilled (`li t0,-1` reloads, a bigger frame) | move_movables (loop.c) hoists less after each insn it moves (`threshold -= 3`): leave statements in the loop for it to move first, so the constant stays "not desirable" (`-dL` prints each decision) | `PogoAI_MoveTowardsTarget` (`gx`/`gz`/`bz` at the loop top) |

Give that block a variable of its own | `PogoAI_MoveTowardsTarget` (`xp`/`xm` per arm, not the mode-5 `k`) |
| `x = (y + 0x800) & 0xFFF` comes out through a temp (`addiu v0,..; andi sN,v0,..`), the target computes in sN (`addiu sN,..; andi sN,sN,..`) | two statements on the variable, `x = y + 0x800; x &= 0xFFF;` (one expression gives the sum its own local pseudo). The extra refs raise x's global.c priority (floor_log2(refs) * refs / live, `decomp.py sched` prints it): keep it between its neighbours' by taking the same number of refs out elsewhere, e.g. a written-out shared tail turned back into a `goto` | `func_800B4AB0` (any 2 of its 9 steering exits as `goto steer`, or all 3 or 4 of one kind, match; 5 or more drop `ang` below `arg0`) |

| A load and an ALU op tie in sched1 (same priority, class 3) and the load wins where you expected source order, or loses where the target has it later | `schedule_select` (sched.c) breaks a same-priority group by `potential_hazard`: when the block has 2+ load/store insns, a load or store beats an ALU op; with only one, the tie falls to luid (the later insn goes later). | `lw a0,X(s); jal` with `addiu a1,s,Y` ahead of the load in the target, and nothing else loads or stores in that block | The arg precomputes (`r = s + Y`) come before the a0 load in RTL but combine folds them into the `$a1 = r` copy after it, and with one memory insn the sched1 tie goes to luid; arg locals, inline helpers, `do {} while (0)`, volatile and a register pin don't change that. Every naturally matched caller with this order has a second load/store in the block. Steering C that flips it: write the call in both arms of the `if` before it, each copy loading the a0 field into a temp and storing it back (`if (c) { f(); s = e->sub; e->sub = s; g(s, ...); } else { s = e->sub; e->sub = s; g(s, ...); }`). The store-back is the second memory insn, but it uses the load, so the load waits out its latency and an insn ready meanwhile fills that slot. The second call sets `$a2` again, so the a2 set loses birthing priority and is that filler, not `addiu a1`; the load then beats `addiu a1` on potential hazard. reload_cse_regs deletes the store and the a0 copy as no-ops, and jump2 cross-jumps the two arms back into the branch-around-call layout. The copies alone leave the swap, and a store-back in one arm only breaks the cross-jump. The temp is needed: a plain `e->sub = e->sub;` never reaches RTL (expand's `store_expr` skips a store whose source rtx equals the target). | A register copy `addu v0,v1,zero` before a multiply chain is missing, where the copy's source dies at the copy (yours runs the chain on the source) | Steering (`func_800B4048`, matched 2026-10-25 with the next row). cse keeps `t = v` only if `t` is canonical in `make_regs_eqv` (its first or last use is outside the extended block) or `v` is set again before the chain. Even then, combine's three-insn combine (copy, shift, minus) folds the copy unless `v` is set between the copy and the chain (`can_combine_p`, `use_crosses_set_p`) or `t` lives on past it. Re-setting `v` after the copy (`t = v; v = (s32)&G; q = (u8 *)v;`) does both, even though combine then folds the re-set into `q` (the folded insn still counts in `use_crosses_set_p`). Where `q` is set then decides where the `lui` lands. Set in the for-init (after `i = 0`), its `lui` ties the hoisted chain at birthing priority in sched1 and loses on luid, so it sits inside `t`'s life and `t` loses v0. Set before the loop (before `i = 0`), its `lo_sum` is the lowest-luid priority-1 leaf, so the pair fills the mult stall before the copy, and sched2 can't move it past the copy (v0 output and anti dependences). Only loop.c's later emissions (strength-reduction inits, a later movable) put the `lui` after a hoisted chain, and that costs one insn before the chain in local-alloc: with 7 insns from the other chain's first insn to this one, the two chains' qtys (11 and 9 refs) tie at `QTY_CMP_PRI` 7500 and the earlier-born one takes v1. A tools/cc1 variant proved that tie was all that was left: with it reversed, the loop-top shape (`t = v; v = (s32)Heroes;`, then in the loop `h = &((HeroInfo *)v)[i]; y = t * 60 / 100; q = D_8005A614;`) byte-matches. The next row wins the tie in C. An inline doesn't help: its argument copy is cse'd away the same way. A dead early `t = 0;` satisfies cse but not combine. Give `t` an earlier set in the same block (`t = a*2+k; v = t & m; ... t = v;`) so local-alloc can't tie it to `v` | `func_800B4048` |

Inside the dividend (`(t * 60 + ((t * 60) & 3)) / 100`) it doesn't: combine's three-insn combination folds the counted pseudo into a new one, and the count goes with it. | A loop counter takes the last callee-saved register (`fp`) where the target spills it beside its bound (`lw t2,0x94(sp)` at the loop top) and keeps a long-lived parameter there (`out`: every use off `fp`; yours reloads it from its argument slot) | Set the counter earlier, e.g. `i = 0;` first in its block as a sibling does. global.c's `allocno_compare` ranks by `floor_log2(refs) * refs / live length`: two more insns of life took `i` from 7/193 (0.073) to 7/195 (0.0718), under `out`'s 11/458 (0.0721). `decomp.py sched` prints the list. The inverse of the `i = 0;` right-before-the-loop row above, for a counter that should lose | `func_800B7D94` |
| A parameter used all through the function takes an early `s` register (`s5`) where the target gives it the last one (`addu fp,a0,zero` first, every index off `fp`), and the pointer locals loaded from it shift up one | Use the parameter itself, not a local copy (`player = arg0;`). assign_parms gives a register parameter a REG_EQUIV to its argument home slot, and local-alloc.c's `update_equiv_regs` doubles the live length of a REG_EQUIV pseudo, which halves its global.c priority; the copy loses the note when cse folds it into the incoming move. If the halved priority now drops below a spilled value (the parameter spills to its home slot), weight a few of its uses: `do { } while (0)` around one `Heroes[player]` lifted 16 refs to 19 | `func_800B980C` |
| A loop counter or angle temp gets another `s` register than the target, whose register for it held unrelated values before (target `s1`: the z target speed, then a hit angle, then a loop counter; `s0`: x target, bounce angle, random pick) | The original reused one local for each such chain of disjoint values: read the target's register sequence and merge the int locals it shares into one variable each. One pseudo gets one register, and the merged variable's extra sets also take away sched1's birthing priority from its first set, so the block order changes too (merging only the counter breaks it; merging both chains matches) | `func_800B980C`; `func_800BC038` (one `v` for every field update, the swarm's `wp` merged in); `PlotUkaPolarMask` (one `ga` for both `get_angle(...) & 0xFFF` results: the second block's other values fill v0, v1 and a0, so the one pseudo is a1 in both); `func_800B4048` (the first loop's score and the tail's scale share v1) |
| A position update stores `x` directly but adjusts `z` by a delta through a reloaded pointer (`sw a0,0x10(v0)` then `lw a1,4(a2)`; `lw a0,0x18(v0)`), and that pointer is in the wrong register (local-alloc gives `v0` to the stored value instead) | Write both halves as the same move by delta: `p->eng->x += new.x - e->x; p->eng->z += new.z - e->z;`. Combine folds `old + (new - old)` to a plain store for `x` (no store in between), but flow has already counted `e`'s extra use, which wins it the register in local-alloc's `qty_compare` (`floor_log2(refs) * refs / life`) | `func_800B980C` |
| A loop rebuilds table bases in its body (`lui`/`addiu` of `D_x` before each use, offsets stepped in an `s` register) where yours hoists them, and a loop-invariant `&Heroes[player]` is computed in the preheader and spilled | Index inside the loop (`T[i].f`, `U[i]`, `&Heroes[player].eng->pos` as an argument) instead of pointers and offsets made before it. loop.c moves the `&Heroes[player]` chain first, and `move_movables` lowers its threshold by 3 for every insn it moves, so the bases after it stay 'not desirable'; `i * size` alone is still strength-reduced | `func_800B980C` |
| A loaded field tested against a constant sits before the block's other loads and fills a load delay (`lw a1,0x64(s4)` early), where the target loads it last into the register its next value uses (`nop`, then `lw v1,0x64(s4)`) | Read the field in the test itself and compute the next value from it in its own statements (`if (ta->X != -1) { d = ta->X - e->a; d = (d << 20) >> 22; ...`), not `if ((d = ta->X) != -1)`. A load into a variable set twice isn't birthing, so sched1 places it before the birthing loads; a single-set temp wins the luid tie and goes last | `func_800B77E8` |
| A store at the join of a two-constant choice is kept before the next block's reload with a `nop` after the reload (`L: sw v0,0x14(s4); lw v1,0xC(s4); nop`), where yours keeps the value in another register and reorg moves the store into a branch delay slot | Write the choice as an `if`/`else` with the store in each arm (`if (w == 3) { t->delay = 0x3C; } else { t->delay = 0xA; }`), not a `?:`. Each store stays in its arm's block, so sched2 can't move it past the reload, and jump2 cross-jumps the two stores into one at the join | `func_800B77E8` |
| A comparison's constant (`li v0,5` before `bne`) is reused for later constants of the same value and lives in an `s` register across calls, where the target loads it locally and starts a fresh register at a later test | If the compare picks an argument, write a call in each arm (`if (h->name == 5) { PlaySfx(0x4CE, ...); } else { PlaySfx(0x4CF, ...); }`). jump1 can't fold the arms into one conditional set, so cse follows the jump into the `else` arm and its path ends at the join label (two ways in), which drops the constant; jump2 then cross-jumps the identical call tails and reorg leaves one `li a0` in the delay slot. The one-set form (`sfx = 0x4CF; if (...) sfx = 0x4CE;`) is a block cse skips | `func_800B77E8` |
| A multiply chain's first step is `sllv v0,v1,v0` by a register that holds 1 (a constant just stored), where yours shifts by an immediate | reload_cse_regs replaces a constant operand with a hard register known to hold the same value. Order the source so that constant's set comes after the loads the chain needs (`f->speedz = ...; f->status = 1;`, not status first): a store before a table load may alias it, which keeps the load after the store and the `li` too early | `func_800B77E8` |
| A difference whose first operand has a constant added comes out with the constant moved onto the second (`lw v0,0x14(v1); addiu v0,v0,-0x180; ...; subu s5,a0,v0`), where the target adds it to the first (`addiu v0,v0,0x180; lw a0,0x14(v1); subu s5,v0,a0`) | Add the constant in its own statement: `y = e->y + 0x180; dy = y - hd->y;`. Inside one expression fold-const.c's `fold` turns `(VAR + CON) - ARG1` into `VAR - (ARG1 - CON)` (its `associate:` case), parentheses or not; it never sees across statements | `func_800BC038` |
| A switch's arms each end in `slt v0,...` and jump to one `bnez v0,L; sh zero,0xE(s4)` at the join, as if a flag were set in each arm and tested once; a flag (`ok = a < b;` per arm, then `if (!ok) t->speed = 0;`) gets the opcodes right but puts the flag and its first operand in v1 | Write the test and its store in each arm: `case 0x80: if (e->x >= t->tx) { t->speed = 0; } break;`. Each arm's compare is then a block-local pseudo that local-alloc puts in v0, so every arm ends in the same `bnez v0; sh`, and jump2 cross-jumps those tails into the last arm's copy. A flag lives across the arms, so global.c picks its register, and expr.c's `do_store_flag` expands the compare's first operand straight into it (its `subtarget`) | `func_800BC038` |
| A difference from a constant (`ang = 0x271 - get_angle(...)`) is written over the constant's register (`subu v1,v1,v0`, then all its uses in v1), where the target writes it over the call's result (`subu v0,v1,v0`) | Use one variable for it and for an unrelated value in another block (here both `get_angle` results share `ang`). local-alloc's `combine_regs` ties a block-local output to a dying input (the `li 0x271`), but refuses an output that lives in several blocks (`reg_qty[sreg] == -1`); global.c then gives the shared variable v0 | `func_800BC038` |
| `try` says MATCH, but LINK differs in the rodata before the function: one jump-table entry points into another arm's code far away (here the function's last tail), where yours points to the `default:` | The original wrote that case out with the same statements as the tail it lands in (case 9's `case 6:` here: `BC038Release(fux); func_8008E27C(...); func_800270B0(arg0); return;`). jump2 cross-jumps the copy into the tail, and jump.c's `tension_vector_labels` (`follow_jumps`) then points the table entry past the jump, straight into the tail | `func_800BC038` |
| Two copies of one tail before jumps to the same label stay apart in the target (`sw v1,0x18(v0); lw a0,0x10(s2); jal SetHeroAction; li a1,0x49; j L` twice), where yours merges one into the other (a `j` into the middle of the first copy) | The target's label is one jump2 made: write every arm's whole tail out (here `if (sfx != NULL) StopSfx(sfx);` in each case, no `goto stop`), so the code at L is a cross-jump of those tails. jump.c compares two jumps to the same label only when the label's `INSN_UID` is below `max_uid`, the bound taken when the pass started; jumps to a label made during the pass are matched only against the code that falls into it, so the copies before them stay | `PlotUkaPolarMask` (case 3) |
| A two-way clamp looks hand-threaded in the target: after an unclamped add it skips the second test, and the second test's load of the field sits before the join label, on the clamp path only (`sw v1,0x1B8(s2); lw a0,0x1B8(s2); L:`); a swarm draft fakes it with a local and a `goto` into the `else` | Write the two plain ifs on the field: `if (s < t) { s += a; if (s > t) s = t; } if (s > t) { s -= a; if (s < t) s = t; }`. jump.c's `thread_jumps` (run just before cse) sends the inner test's false branch past the second test, which compares the same values on that path, and reorg's `fill_slots_from_thread` moves the first test's branch target past the second test's load, which `redundant_insn` finds already in a0 | `PlotUkaPolarMask` |
| A store sits after a later statement's table load in the target (`lh v1,2(v1); sw zero,0x1B8(s2)`), where yours has it before (right after the first load), and a global's `lui`/`lw` lands elsewhere among the same insns | Put the store after that statement in the source (`ext->unk1D8 = tbl[a].x ...; D_800D5490++; ext->unk1B8 = 0;`). sched.c's `true_dependence` can't tell two struct references at varying addresses apart (`/s` in the dumps: the table entry and the ext field), so sched1 keeps their source order; a scalar global at a fixed address never conflicts with them, so only its luid ties place it | `PlotUkaPolarMask` |
| `slt v0,v0,s0; xori v0,v0,1; beqz v0` for an `a <= b` test (yours `slt; bnez`) | The source stored the comparison: `s32 brake = abs(d) <= abs(v) * 8; if (brake)`. expr.c's `do_store_flag` computes `<=` as the inverse `slt` plus `xori 1`, and the branch then tests the flag; a plain `if (a <= b)` branches on the `slt` | `PlotUkaPolarMask` |

## Classify the diff, then work through the list

Classify the diff by what differs, then try the items for that kind in
order. Note which ones you tried.

**Register-only** (same opcodes, operands differ):
1. Add or remove a temp.
2. Split or merge an expression.
3. `x += y` vs `x = x + y`.
4. Signedness and width of locals.
5. Declaration order of locals.
6. A cast on an intermediate value.
7. Operand order of a commutative operator.
8. Then read the [cc1 dump](#why-does-a-register-differ) and the rule that
   picked the register ([Reading the compiler's rules](#reading-the-compilers-rules)).
   Try the candidate shapes in a [test file](#using-the-compiler-directly)
   before rebuilding the function for each one.

**Instruction order** (same instructions, different order):
1. Statement order.
2. Chained (`a = b = 1`) vs separate assignments.
3. Reading a global into a local once vs reading it twice.
4. `if (a && b)` vs nested ifs.
5. The declared type of the globals involved.
6. `TRUE` vs `1`: no codegen effect, so rule it out quickly.
7. Then read sched1's and sched2's traces (`-dS`, `-dR`) and the scheduling
   rules ([Reading the compiler's rules](#reading-the-compilers-rules)). A
   load moved across a store is often [aliasing](#reading-the-compilers-rules)
   (Memory dependences), not priority.

**Opcode or length** (different instructions or count):
1. Loop form (see [Diff patterns](#diff-patterns)).
2. `/` vs `>>`.
3. `(u32)` casts.
4. Unsigned vs signed compares.
5. Early return vs if/else.
6. Macros (Sony's, or a repo macro in `include/`).
7. `s16`/`u16` locals vs `s32` with casts: a short is extended where it's read,
   not where it's set (Short locals in [Reading the compiler's
   rules](#reading-the-compilers-rules)).
8. Then compile the candidate shapes in a [test file](#using-the-compiler-directly)
   and dump `.rtl` for the one that comes closest.

## Why does a register differ?

Start with `tools/dk python3 tools/scripts/decomp.py vars NAME`. For each
block `regions` says differs, it lists the register swaps (compiled `a1`
where the target has `v1`), the C variables cc1 put in the compiled
register (from the ECOFF `.def` lines of a `-g` build), and of those the
ones the block's C lines name (`--all` lists every variable's register or
stack slot). When one variable wants a different register in several
blocks, it says so and points to `splitvars`.

For the reason behind a choice, dump the compiler's register allocation for
the file:

```sh
tools/dk sh -c 'mipsel-linux-gnu-cpp -P -nostdinc -undef -Iinclude -Iext/psyq47/include -D__GNUC__=2 -D_LANGUAGE_C -DPSX -DGVAL=0 src/main/main.c > build/x.i && /opt/gcc/2.8.1-psx/cc1 -quiet -O2 -G0 -fno-builtin -dl -dg -o build/x.s build/x.i'
```

Run it with the function's C in place of its `INCLUDE_ASM` line (swap in the
file of the function's segment for `src/main/main.c`). It writes
`build/x.i`, `build/x.s`, `build/x.i.lreg` and `build/x.i.greg`; `build/`
is gitignored, so nothing needs deleting (swarm workers can't run `rm`).

- `build/x.i.lreg` (local-alloc) lists each pseudo's uses and lifetime, then
  "Register N in M" for the pseudos it placed, then the RTL with `REG_DEAD`
  notes. It shows which pseudo was tied to which (an insn's result shares
  the register of a source operand that dies there).
- `build/x.i.greg` lists "Register dispositions" for pseudos global.c placed.
- "pref HI_REG" marks `mfhi` results: local-alloc skips them and global.c
  picks the register, avoiding registers other pseudos prefer (a return
  value prefers v0).

For branch layout differences, dump the jump passes instead (`-dj -dJ -dd`
write `build/x.i.jump`, `build/x.i.jump2` and `build/x.i.dbr`) to see which pass fixes the
layout. Add `-fno-schedule-insns2` to tell register choice apart from sched2's
reordering.

For a gcc 2.7.2 unit (`CC_VER = 2.7.2-psx`), run `/opt/gcc/2.7.2-psx/cc1` instead (with the
unit's `EXTRA_CC1FLAGS`). For why a pass made its choice, in either compiler, read its source:
[Reading the compiler's rules](#reading-the-compilers-rules).

## Using the compiler directly

The container has the real compilers (`/opt/gcc/2.8.1-psx/cc1`, `/opt/gcc/2.7.2-psx/cc1`).
Use them for more than the final diff:
- dump any pass for the function;
- follow one value through the passes;
- compile small test files to see what a C shape becomes.

This is how `PlotBall` (5576 B) went from 777 differing instructions to a match with no permuter
with natural C. Do it before guessing at C, and before the permuter.

The flags are the unit's: `-O2 -G0 -fno-builtin` for gcc 2.8.1 code (`configs/USA/project.mk`).
Units with their own settings set `CC_VER` and `EXTRA_CC1FLAGS` in the Makefile. Leaving out
`-fno-builtin` inlines `abs` and changes the code.

The game's own files were C++ (`PANEL.CPP`, `PAUSE.CPP`: PROTOTYPES.md, Jampack), and the
container also has `/opt/gcc/2.8.1-psx/cc1plus`. On C-style code it emits the same code as cc1
(tested on `func_8007F314`), so a C shape stays the thing to change. A C++ idiom can explain
odd C, though: `xori v0,v0,1` after a call is `!f()` on a `bool`, which C writes as `r ^= 1`.

`tools/cc1/` rebuilds that cc1 from source, byte for byte ([tools/cc1/README.md](../tools/cc1/README.md)).
A variant is the same compiler with one thing changed: a pass that prints its decisions, a
decision forced the other way, other host flags (`tools/cc1/build.sh NAME --patch F --cflags
"..."`). `tools/cc1/with.sh NAME COMMAND` runs `try --file`, `sched` or the dump loop below with
it, `tools/cc1/check.sh NAME` builds the whole game with it, and `tools/cc1/with.sh NAME python3
tools/scripts/cc1_compare.py FUNC [FILE...]` lists which builds of a function it changes. Use
one to prove which decision a stuck function needs before searching for the C that gives it;
the build never uses a variant. The host's x87 precision is ruled out (README there).

**1. Dump every pass of the function.** `decomp.py sched NAME` writes the file's preprocessed
source, with the C as it is now, to `build/sched/NAME.i`. Run cc1 on it with the dump flags,
then cut the function's section out of each dump (each holds the whole file). The loop runs in
Git Bash on the host:

```sh
tools/dk python3 tools/scripts/decomp.py sched NAME >/dev/null
tools/dk sh -c 'cd build/sched && /opt/gcc/2.8.1-psx/cc1 -quiet -O2 -G0 -fno-builtin -dr -ds -dc -dl -dg -dS -dR -dJ -o NAME.s NAME.i'
for k in rtl cse combine lreg greg sched sched2 jump2; do
  awk -v f=NAME '$0 == ";; Function " f {p = 1} p && /^;; Function / && $0 != ";; Function " f {exit} p' build/sched/NAME.i.$k > build/sched/NAME.$k
done
```

The flags and their files are in the [table below](#reading-the-compilers-rules).

**2. Find the pass that decides it.** Look up one instruction of the differing block (a call, a
field offset) in each dump, in pass order:
- `.rtl` (expand): the pseudos the C itself makes, before any optimisation;
- `.cse`, then `.combine`: what got substituted or merged;
- `.lreg`, then `.greg`: which register each pseudo got.

The first pass where your RTL can't give the target's code is the one to change the C for. No
dumps exist for the target, so reason backwards from its asm to the RTL it needs. For example,
`move s3,v0` then `sll v0,v0,16` means the shift reads a temporary that holds the call's result
and dies in v0, not the variable in s3. If `.rtl` has no such temp (`var = v0` straight from
the call), the C has to create one: the rules below say what does.

`decomp.py trace NAME --insn UID` does this lookup for one insn. It dumps every pass and prints
the insn in each, with `=` where a pass left it alone. It names the passes that moved it (loop.c
hoists under a new uid, which it follows), rewrote it or deleted it, and the insns a deleted
insn went into (combine's merges, cse's replacements). The sched2 dump also shows what
reload_cse_regs did after greg's dump (sched2 itself drops only no-op moves), and the trace says
so. `--var V` (or `--reg N`) lists every insn
that mentions a variable's pseudo, each with what became of it. The pseudo comes from
declaration order and is checked against the `-g` home. `--file F.c` traces a test copy, as
`try --file` compiles it. On `func_800B4048`'s copy `t = v` it shows in one call what took a
session by hand: flow deletes the dead early `t = 0`, then combine deletes the copy into the
`*60` chain's shift and minus.

Besides RTL, the dumps print:
- `.cse`: `;; Processing block from A to B, N sets.` for each pass over an extended block (A is
  its first insn's uid, B the insn it stops at). One block shows up once per branch path it
  follows. The last pass over an insn sets its operands.
- `.lreg`: `Register N used R times across L insns in block B; crosses C call` per pseudo, the
  refs and life behind both allocators' priorities. "in block B" means it is local-alloc's to
  place.
- `.greg`: `;; N regs to allocate:` in priority order, then `;; N conflicts: ...` and
  `;; N preferences: ...` per pseudo, then `Register dispositions`. Numbers below 32 in a
  conflict list are hard registers live during that pseudo's life (16 is s0, taken by a pseudo
  local-alloc placed). `decomp.py sched NAME` prints the same allocation order with each
  pseudo's priority and the C variables in its register.

**3. Try C shapes in a test file.** Rebuilding the overlay takes about 30 s for every try, and
the rest of the function hides the effect. Put the snippet in a small file instead, with only
what affects it: the calls around it, the branch it sits in, its struct offsets. Then compile
several shapes at once and compare the instructions in question:

```sh
mkdir -p scratch/t    # scratch/ is git-ignored
# scratch/t/head.h: the externs and structs (or #include project headers; see below)
# scratch/t/v1.c, v2.c, ...: #include "head.h" and one shape each
for v in v1 v2 v3; do
  tools/dk sh -c "cd scratch/t && mipsel-linux-gnu-cpp -P $v.c > $v.i && /opt/gcc/2.8.1-psx/cc1 -quiet -O2 -G0 -fno-builtin -o $v.s $v.i" </dev/null
  echo "== $v"; grep -A8 "jal.*func_800154A0" scratch/t/$v.s
done
```

To use project headers, run cpp with the Makefile's `CPPFLAGS` (`-P -nostdinc -undef -Iinclude
-Iext/psyq47/include -D__GNUC__=2 -D_LANGUAGE_C -DPSX -DGVAL=0`) from the repo root. Compare
patterns, not register numbers: which register an instruction reads, whether a `move` exists,
the order. Add `-dr -ds` to a test compile to see why.

To test a whole function the same way, `decomp.py try NAME --file scratch/t/v1.c` compiles the
file (a copy of the function after its unit's `#include` lines) with the unit's settings,
Makefile overrides included, and diffs NAME from it as `try` does, in about 2 s: the unit is
not edited or rebuilt and no attempt is recorded (`func_800BE914`'s variants went this way).
Give several files (`--file scratch/t/v*.c`) to compile them in parallel: `try` ranks them by
the regions score (a match, then exact instructions, matching blocks, paired instructions, then
fewer differing ones) and shows the best one's diff. Six of `func_800B4048`'s variants took
2.3 s together.

When the diff is an order or allocation tie that the order of independent statements decides
(sched1's luid ties follow the source, and the lives they give decide local-alloc's registers),
`decomp.py orders NAME --lines A-B [--count 150]` searches it. It splits those lines (inside one
block) into statements and keeps every pair that must stay in order: a variable written and read
or written, memory written and read (stores through `->`, `[`, `*`, globals), any call. Since any
two memory accesses count as meeting, a run of stores keeps its source order; `--free-memory`
compares them by place instead (a global, or the fields and indexes from a name), so stores to
different globals, fields or pointers may swap. It then
compiles that many random orders as copies of the unit, ranks them as `try --file` does and
prints the best order. Run it again from the best (`--file` with a copy, another `--seed`) to
refine. Explain the winner by its rule before committing it. `func_800B8294` matched this way:
two runs over block 0 (280 orders, about 30 s) found the order that puts the first offset's `*15`
chain in a0.

Then port the winning shape and check it in the function. Allocation priorities depend on the
whole function (each variable's refs and live range), so a shape that fixes the snippet can
move other variables. In `PlotBall`, an `s16` fixed the snippet but shifted every s-register,
until splitting `done` into the target's two variables put them back. When that happens,
compare the `.greg` allocation order with yours: the target's registers tell you which pseudo
must come first.

Writing scripts: `tools/dk python3 - <<'EOF' </dev/null` runs nothing, because the
`</dev/null` replaces the heredoc. Write the script to a file in `scratch/` and run that.

## Reading the compiler's rules

Some diffs are a tie inside one pass: two instructions in the other order,
two values in each other's registers, or a shared tail merged into the wrong
copy. For these, read the rule that breaks the tie instead of guessing at C:

1. Dump the pass that decides it (table below), as in [Using the compiler
   directly](#using-the-compiler-directly).
2. Find the tie in the dump's trace. For register allocation and scheduling,
   `decomp.py sched NAME [--pass sched|sched2] [--block N] [--insn UIDS]` does steps 1-2:
   global.c's allocation order with each pseudo's refs, live length, priority, register and
   C variables, then each block in final order. A block's header counts its memory-unit
   insns (loads and stores; `potential hazard on` at 2+), each insn is tagged `ld`/`st`/`mul`,
   and its line names the rule that took it off the ready list ahead of the runner-up, in
   sched.c's order: `block end` (tail priority), `only insn ready`, `priority`, `birthing
   priority` (`7f000001`, from `adjust_priority`), `class` (the insn scheduled just before uses
   the runner-up's load), `greater potential hazard` (`schedule_select` overriding the rank
   order), or a `tie ... luid` (the later insn in the RTL stays later, with why the hazard
   didn't decide). `order from T-n` means no insn joined the list since that step, so sched.c
   didn't re-sort and the pair keeps that step's order. For func_800BC274's
   `jal func_8001CD04` block (its C before the fix) it printed `100 ... tie with 98 at priority 1: luid ... (only 1
   memory insn in the block: no potential hazard)`. The rules are read from the dump's ready
   lists, RTL and LOG_LINKS; the potential-hazard model (a store over a load over an ALU insn,
   at 2+ memory insns) predicted every tie of five units' dumps, but mult/div against memory
   insns isn't modelled. A parked function's C under `#ifdef NON_MATCHING` is compiled with
   `-DNON_MATCHING` (`regions` and `vars` do the same).
3. Read the function that decides it in `tools/reference/gcc-2.8.1/` (FSF 2.8.1, the base of
   our `2.8.1-psx` cc1) or, for the two gcc 2.7.2 units, `tools/reference/gcc-2.7.2.3/`. The
   2.7.2.3 `README.decomp.md` says which file holds which pass; the 2.8.1 one lists what was
   checked to be the same.
4. Work through the rule by hand, and change the C so the tie goes the target's way.

Do this before the permuter. It matched `UpdateBigScore` (7/307 off) after six swarm sessions and
a permuter run had made no progress.

| Flag | File | Shows |
|---|---|---|
| `-dr` | `.rtl` | expand: the RTL straight from the C, so which pseudos and copies the C itself makes |
| `-dj` | `.jump` | the first jump pass |
| `-ds` | `.cse` | after cse, with a `Processing block` line per pass over each extended block |
| `-dL` | `.loop` | after loop.c (hoisted invariants) |
| `-dt` | `.cse2` | after the second cse |
| `-df` | `.flow` | after flow: liveness, `REG_DEAD` notes |
| `-dc` | `.combine` | after combine: merged insns, copies folded away |
| `-dS` | `.sched` | sched1's trace per block: each insn's priority, then `ready list at T-n` cycle by cycle from the block end (`7f000001` is birthing priority; "launching X with N stalls"; "greater potential hazard") |
| `-dR` | `.sched2` | the same for sched2, after register allocation |
| `-dl` | `.lreg` | local-alloc: refs, lifetime and deaths per pseudo, then `Register N in M` |
| `-dg` | `.greg` | the RTL after reload, the order sched2 starts from |
| `-dJ` | `.jump2` | the RTL after jump2's cross-jumping |
| `-dd` | `.dbr` | the RTL after delay-slot filling |

The rules found so far, each one checked against a trace:

- **Expansion (`expand_call` in `calls.c`, `store_expr` in `expr.c`).**
  - `var = f(...)`, with `var` in the call's mode, copies v0 straight into
    `var`. There's no temp in between (`.rtl`: `(set (reg/v var) (reg v0))`).
  - A temp of the call's value (`copy_to_reg (valreg)`) appears when the
    result is converted: an `int` result into an `s16` variable. It also
    appears when the call is an operand of an expression or an argument of
    an inlined function.
  - `integrate.c` copies each argument of an inlined function into a new
    pseudo for the parameter, unless the parameter is `const`. That copy is
    the extra `move a0,v1` an inline leaves behind (`PlotBall`).
- **Short locals (gcc 2.8.1).** An `s16`/`u16` local is a 16-bit pseudo that
  isn't extended when it's set, only when it's read.
  - The marks of one in the target: a raw `addiu` increment, `sll`/`sra 16`
    before each use, and `sll 16; bgtz` or `sll 16; beqz` tests.
  - An `s32` local with `(s16)` casts gives the same shifts, but its refs and
    live range differ, so allocation differs.
- **16-bit arithmetic (`convert_to_integer` in `convert.c`, `fold` in
  `fold-const.c`).** When a result is stored to a 16-bit field or masked, the
  front end does the `+`/`-` in 16 bits.
  - A constant becomes its 16-bit unsigned value: `(u16)x - 0xE3` is
    `+ 0xFF1D`, loaded with `ori`.
  - fold then moves constants outward through the sum. A cast is the only
    way to keep the constant with one operand.
- **CSE's canonical register (`make_regs_eqv` in `cse.c`).**
  - After `b = a`, every later use of either is rewritten to the class's
    first register.
  - The new register `b` becomes first when it lives beyond the extended
    block being processed and its last use is later than the current first's.
    "Beyond the block" means its first use is before the block or its last use
    after it (`cse_basic_block_start`/`_end`).
  - So a variable that lives on makes the uses read the variable, and a temp
    used only nearby stays first. With follow-jumps, the pass over one branch
    path can end before a join that the variable is used after (`.cse`'s
    `Processing block` lines).
  - `PlotBall`'s `abs((s16)k)` reads the call's temp in v0, not `k`'s s3,
    because `k` is an `s16` (the temp exists) and only the temp is first.
  - "Last use" is `REGNO_LAST_UID`, which `reg_scan` sets before each cse
    pass: the last insn that mentions the register, including an assignment
    that flow later deletes as dead. So a dead `p = NULL;` after the real
    last uses makes `p` outlive the registers copied from it
    (`PlotScriptFX`).
  - The copy swap (`cse_insn`, "Special handling for (set REG0 REG1)"): when
    `b = a` makes `b` first and the insn just before it (notes skipped) sets
    `a`, cse rewrites the pair as `b = <a's value>; a = b`. An insn between
    them stops it.
- **CSE's block ends (`cse_end_of_basic_block`, `cse_insn` in `cse.c`;
  `toplev.c`).**
  - cse1 ends an extended block at a `NOTE_INSN_LOOP_END`, so the code after
    a `do { } while (0)` is cse'd knowing nothing from before it. cse2 runs
    `after_loop` and reads straight through.
  - So an assignment that repeats the value a register got before such a
    note survives cse1, and cse2 turns it into a self-copy (`src_related =
    dest`). Only cse1 is followed by `delete_dead_from_cse`, so flow counts
    the copy's set and use. Combine then folds the first set into the copy,
    and the counted refs stay: `v = k; do {} while (0); v = k;` gives `v` two
    more refs (more with loop depth) and no code, and the set that is left
    sits right after the loop notes (`PogoAI_RadialSearch`).
- **Combine (`combine.c`).** `+` becomes `|` when combine can prove the two
  operands share no set bits (`nonzero_bits`). For a register set in several
  places, that's the union over all its sets (`set_nonzero_bits_and_sign_copies`).
  - `i * 5` scaled as `(i << 2) | i` rather than `addu` means combine knew
    `i`'s bits.
  - `PlotBall`'s side index used `or` with an `i = 0 / 1 / 3 / ...` chain and
    `addu` with an inline that `return`s each value.
  - It folds `a = a + N` into a following `b = a` only when `a` dies there
    (`added_sets_2` in `try_combine`). A later use of `a` keeps the two insns
    (`addiu v0,v0,N; move sN,v0`), and local-alloc's `optimize_reg_copy_1`
    can still move that use to `b` afterwards (`PlotScriptFX`).
- **Scheduling (`sched.c`, both passes).** It schedules backwards from the
  end of the block.
  - `priority` is the longest chain of predecessors, adding latency minus 1
    per link, so most insns get 1 to 3.
  - `rank_for_schedule` picks the ready insn with, in order: the higher
    priority; the better class against the insn just scheduled (independent,
    or latency 1, wins); the later position in the RTL. A tie therefore keeps
    the original order.
  - `schedule_select`: among equal priority, the first insn with the largest
    potential hazard goes first. It is 0 for every memory insn unless the
    block has 2+ loads and stores; then a store beats a load and either beats
    an ALU insn. A mult/div insn always beats an ALU insn.
  - An insn that is ready while a load waits out its latency fills that slot.
    So a load whose result another insn in the block uses lands at least two
    slots before that user, and whatever is ready then goes in between. An insn
    with birthing priority (next bullet) is placed as soon as it is ready
    instead. Taking that priority away (a second set) can make it the filler
    (`func_800BC274`).
  - In sched1 only, `adjust_priority` and `birthing_insn_p` give launch
    priority to an insn that sets a register set only once and still live.
    It then lands right before its first use. A register set twice doesn't
    get this (Diff patterns: `UpdateBigScore`, `func_800926CC`).
  - Loop notes from `do { } while (0)` are a barrier: nothing moves across
    them. In `sched_analyze_insn` the first insn after the notes depends on
    every insn before it, and every later insn depends on it, with that
    insn's cost. A load there raises every later insn's priority by one,
    which can turn their order into a tie.
  - An insn with no consumer in its block (`i = 0` before a loop, a copy
    into a variable used only later) keeps priority 1 and lands at the top
    of the block. A loop note after it gives it a consumer, the barrier insn,
    so it moves down to the barrier (`func_80093854`).
- **Loop rotation (`expand_end_loop` in `stmt.c`, then jump).** A `for` or
  `while` test at the top moves to the bottom, and jump copies it in front of
  the loop (folded away after `i = 0`). The target's test-at-top shape with
  a `j` back is `while (1) { if (...) break; ... }` (still a loop to loop.c,
  `func_8008CFBC`, `PlotScriptFX`), a `for (;;)` whose body declares a
  variable before the test (`stmt.c`'s scan stops at the block note), or a
  `goto` loop (no loop notes, so no hoisting and no weighting).
- **Loop invariants (`loop.c`).**
  - `scan_loop` skips a "phony" loop, one whose first insn after the start
    note isn't a label. A `do { } while (0)` that starts with a statement is
    one, since jump deletes its unused start label. flow still weights its
    refs by depth (`func_80093854`).
  - It also skips registers that loop optimization made for an inner loop.
  - `combine_movables` merges identical invariants (two `lui %hi(X)`),
    adding their savings and lifetimes. `move_movables` moves one when
    threshold × savings × lifetime is at least the loop's insn count. The
    threshold is near the register count, so nearly every invariant moves.
  - A hoisted constant that gets no register (more call-crossing pseudos than
    `s` registers) is rematerialized by reload at each use.
- **Memory dependences (`true_dependence`, `memrefs_conflict_p` in `sched.c`).**
  - A register set once to a symbol stands for that symbol (`canon_rtx`).
    Two different symbols never conflict, so stores through such pointers
    don't keep a load of another global below them.
  - A pointer set twice is unknown, so its stores do keep the load below them.
  - A struct field at a varying address can't conflict with a scalar at a
    fixed address. An array element counts as a struct field. So when the
    target keeps a global's load below a field store, the original read the
    global as an aggregate member: a field of its struct or an array element
    (`PlotBall`: `D_8005A648` as `D_8005A614`'s field `0x34`, the hook
    `D_8005AA94` as `D_8005AA70[9]`). That's instead of a pointer temp to
    the field.
- **Local register allocation (`local-alloc.c`).**
  - It only handles pseudos used in one block that die once. `REG_DEAD` and
    `REG_UNUSED` both count as deaths; the rest go to `global.c`.
  - `qty_compare` gives each one priority refs × floor_log2(refs) / life.
    Refs are flow's counts weighted by loop depth; life is measured in half-insns.
  - Equal priorities go to the pseudo born first. Each takes the first
    register in `REG_ALLOC_ORDER` (v0, v1, a0, ...) that is free over its whole life.
  - Two outputs of one insn, like a divide's quotient and remainder, are born
    together. `note_stores` walks the PARALLEL backwards, so the remainder
    gets the lower qty number and wins a tie. The unused remainder lives one
    half-insn; at loop depth 2 the quotient ties it when one insn sits
    between the divide and the quotient's use, and then takes a0, not v0
    (`PlotScriptFX`).
  - `combine_regs` ties an insn's output to a register operand that dies
    there, so they share a register. The operand must not be inside a
    memory address, and the output must be local and not yet placed.
  - `optimize_reg_copy_1` (in `update_equiv_regs`): for a copy `dest = src`
    where src lives on, it rewrites src's later uses in the block to dest, up
    to src's death. Only uses after the copy in sched1's order count
    (`func_80093854`: the loads use `g` because sched1 puts `g = a` at the top).
- **Global register allocation (`global.c`).**
  - `allocno_compare` uses the same priority, refs × floor_log2(refs) / live
    length, from the `-dl` dump's "used N times across M insns". Equal
    priorities go to the lower pseudo number. In that order each one takes
    the first free register, so for two pseudos that cross calls, the first
    takes the lower `s` register (`func_8008C824`).
  - Preferences (`set_preference`): an insn `dest = (op src ...)` gives src a
    preference for dest's hard register, including a local temp already placed
    in an argument register (`sll a3,b,15` before `sra a3,a3,16`).
    `find_reg` takes a preferred register over the one it found if it is free.
    `prune_preferences` first drops any preferred register that is a hard
    conflict, i.e. live as a hard register during the pseudo's life. A
    lower-priority neighbour's preference keeps a register out of pass 0
    only if the pseudo doesn't prefer it too (`func_80078DD4`).
  - A pseudo conflicts with every hard register that local-alloc gave a
    pseudo live during its life (the low numbers in `.greg`'s `;; N
    conflicts:` list). A local tie therefore changes which `s` register a
    global pseudo can take: in `PlotBall`, `dz` tied into s0 kept `dx` out of
    s0 (Diff patterns: delta loads).
  - Refs are flow's counts, taken before combine (`flow.c`, one per register
    occurrence in an insn, times the loop depth between the notes); combine
    doesn't lower them.
  - The live length is sched1's count of insns (the `-dS` dump's "life
    shortened from N to M"). sched1 leaves a parameter's entry move out of
    the count.
  - `update_equiv_regs` (`local-alloc.c`) then doubles the live length of a
    pseudo set once to a constant, such as a `%hi` that loop.c hoisted. That
    halves its priority (`func_80095258`).
  - loop.c puts hoisted insns just before the loop's start note, so inside
    an enclosing `do { } while (0)` they keep the outer depth
    (`func_80095258`).
- **Cross-jumping (`find_cross_jump`, `do_cross_jump` in `jump.c`, jump2).**
  - The pass walks forward. A jump hands its tail to the first matching
    copy it finds among the jumps to the same label, most recently threaded
    first: `redirect_jump` moves a threaded jump to the head of its new
    label's chain, and the search starts at the head. Jumps already aimed at
    the label when jump2 starts sit in code order, the last one at the head.
  - That needs the matched insns to follow a label, or a branch around the
    jump when the other copy is also preceded by a jump (the codes must
    agree before the branch-around discount applies: `find_cross_jump`
    stops without it when one side is a jump and the other an insn, call
    or barrier; labels are skipped on the other copy's side). A tail that
    follows a call hands itself over only to a copy with the same call
    before it, arguments and all.
  - So a first copy that follows a branch around it (`if (c) { x = 0;
    break; }`) is kept when no other copy in the chain follows a
    conditional branch; the last copy, which follows a label, then hands
    itself to it at the end of the pass, and the rest follow in the next
    pass. That keeps the earliest copy, as retail's `func_8007F314` does.
    Any later exit shaped `if (c) { x = 0; break; }` captures the first
    copy instead: write it as `if (!c) { ...; x = 0; } else x = 0;` or let
    it fall to an exit after the block.
  - Jumps to a label made during jump2 itself are not searched (`INSN_UID < max_uid`).
  - Before reload, threading stops at a loop's start note (`follow_jumps`).
    An empty loop after a switch therefore makes jump2 thread the `break`s
    one at a time, so each tail meets the copies threaded before it, and the
    first copy is kept. Without that loop every `break` already targets the
    join and all merge into the last tail. It applies to every family of
    tails at once; `func_8007F314` needed the first copy kept only for its
    `ret = 0` exits, which the shape above gives.
  - `decomp.py jtrace NAME [--cross]` runs cc1 under ptrace and lists each
    comparison (`fcj E1 vs E2 min N -> F1 F2`), each cross-jump and each
    redirect by insn uid, annotated from the `.greg` dump.
  - Each jump is threaded before its cross-jump is tried, and
    `redirect_jump` deletes the old label once its last user has gone. So a
    label that stops a tail's match lasts only until its users are
    threaded, often in the same pass. A copy that has collected others has
    a label before its first insn, so in a later pass it can hand itself
    over too.
  - Two tails that end up jumping to a label jump2 made (the label before
    a kept copy) are never compared again, so their own preceding code (a
    call and its arguments) stays duplicated. That keeps two identical
    tails apart when their last statement is a copy that gives itself up
    first (`func_8007F314`'s cases 0x40 and 0x80 end `if (...) f(); ret =
    0;`, so their jumps reach the new label before they meet), and it
    splits them when they should merge (two `PlaySfx(); ret = 0;` tails
    that each follow a label).
  - A layout `x; L: j T` with `x` in other jumps' delay slots (`j L; x`)
    comes from a cross-jump that put `L` before one shared insn, which
    reorg then moved into the `j`'s slot (`SetTweakAi`,
    `AR_PI_TestOutOfBounds`).
- **Delay slots (`reorg.c`).**
  - `fill_simple_delay_slots` fills from before the branch first, then
    fills an unconditional jump from its target. Its backward scan stops at
    a label, so a jump with an existing label right before it (from jump2)
    keeps the insn before that label and takes its slot from the target.
  - `steal_delay_list_from_fallthrough` moves a filled jump's slot into the
    branch just before it.
  - `redundant_insn` lets a branch skip a target insn that its delay slot
    already executes.

## The validation gate (`done`)

`done` records nothing, and commits nothing, unless every check passes:

| Check | Fails when |
|---|---|
| Scope | the working tree replaces any other function's `INCLUDE_ASM` (one function per commit), or changes another function's C on a line that doesn't call this one (fixing calls to it is allowed; 2026-10-19) |
| Still asm | the function's own `INCLUDE_ASM` line is still in its file (its asm would "match" itself; `try` prints `STILL-ASM`) |
| Lint | the function still contains `M2C_*` macros or m2c's `?` type, or any inline asm (instructions, a register pin, an empty barrier), in its body or in a macro or static inline it uses (the SDK's `gte_*` macros excepted) |
| Function | the function's compiled words differ from the target |
| Full build | a clean rebuild of every target (exe and overlays) is not byte-identical |

Then it records the match, copies the same C to identical functions elsewhere
(they're listed in the commit body), refreshes the dashboard, and commits
`src/ include/ configs/ .decomp/ docs/` as `Match NAME (seg, N bytes)` with a
`Validated-by:` trailer.

**Enforcement:** the pre-commit hook (`tools/hooks/pre-commit`, enable it with
`git config core.hooksPath tools/hooks`) refuses any commit that replaces an
`INCLUDE_ASM` line unless the commit came from `done`.

## Commits that aren't matches

Renames (`decomp.py rename`), header moves, typing an already matched function,
and file splits are ordinary small commits made after `tools/dk make verify`
prints `OK` for every target. Keep each one to a single purpose.

## GTE code

Write GTE code with Sony's macros from `inline_c.h` and `gtemac.h` (PsyQ 4.7,
included by `include/psyq.h`): `gte_ldv0(v); gte_rt(); gte_stlvl(out);` is
`lwc2 x2, nop, nop, mvmva, swc2 x3`. Read the target's cop2 instruction to pick
the macro: `rtps`, `nclip`, `avsz3`, and `mvmva sf, mx, v, cv, lm`, where mx
0/1/2 = RT/LL/LC, v 0..2 = V0..V2 (3 = IR), cv 0/1/3 = TR/BK/none
(`mvmva 1,0,0,0,0` is `gte_rt`/`gte_rtv0tr`). The macros emit placeholder
words; `tools/scripts/dmpsx.py` turns them into the real instructions after
cc1, as Sony's DMPSX did. The gate's lint doesn't count these macros as
inline asm. m2c can't draft GTE functions, so write them from the asm.

## Loops gcc rotates but the target doesn't

gcc 2.8 moves a `while`/`for` loop's exit test to the bottom when that test
jumps to the loop's own end. If the target tests at the top and jumps back
with `j` at the bottom, the source left the loop by `return` or by `goto` to
a label after it:

```c
for (;;) {
    if (--n == -1) {
        goto none; /* or return */
    }
    ...
}
```

A loop built from labels and `goto` alone doesn't match: loop.c then skips it,
so loop constants (`li $a0, -1`) aren't hoisted.

## For AI agents

- Work on one function at a time and commit it before picking the next.
- Start each session by reading [AGENT_LOG.md](AGENT_LOG.md). End it by
  updating its current state, next steps and open leads (what got parked and
  why), and adding a short session-log entry. Idioms you learned go in
  [Diff patterns](#diff-patterns).
- Never fake a match: no whole-function `asm()`, no editing `asm/`, `build/`
  or the checksums, and no committing around the gate.
- The project skill `.claude/skills/match-function` runs this loop: `/match-function`
  matches the next function, `/match-function NAME` a specific one.
